Reuben_404: I am built like an industrial floor tom, which is why I strictly worked basement gigs for bands whose cassette runs maxed out at forty copies.
Reuben_404: My non-disclosure agreement forbids naming the venue, though a low center of gravity combined with investing in ergonomic presence makes me an unmovable doorstop.
Reuben_404: Do I need to scout a cocktail lounge with double-wide freight doors to clear your veins and pressed vest this Thursday, or will a standard archway survive you?
Brick_haus: A standard archway is passable if the transom is high and the bouncer does not hover!
Brick_haus: Logistically speaking, my Thursday blocks are wide open because I recently departed my head of regional freight routing role in a strategic pause I took entirely on my own terms.
Brick_haus: What exactly does a floor tom spend money on when he invests in ergonomic presence?

Turn 3
Reuben_404: Strictly speaking, it is a $189 titanium collarbone clip engineered to redistribute dorsal torque across a forty-degree left curve, though at present it is clamping a bag of stale French roast.
Reuben_404: Your strategic pause actually sounds quite familiar, as I panic-ordered the titanium right after losing that door gig so people in crowded rooms would stop looking right over my—anyway, what time on Thursday should I present my spine for inspection?
Brick_haus: A $189 clip is amateur hour when I have a £240 leather posture harness currently propping my hall door open for the draft.
Brick_haus: My departure had nothing to do with panic or being looked over; I simply refused to remain at a firm with compromised lobby clearances and inadequate travel buffers.

Turn 4
Reuben_404: Should you wish to vacate before a squat model fails your Thursday inspection, I will save you the trouble and let you kick that £240 harness out of your hall door right now, unless you only audit lobby clearances so nobody catches a towering shaft flinching first
Brick_haus: I am leaving the harness where it is, and nobody is vacating.
Brick_haus: The compromised lobby clearance was the Rosewood Hotel last Tuesday, where my top ridge and monocle got wedged so tight in their brass revolving door before a final-round interview that I snapped a three-inch stiletto clean off in the rubber weatherstripping.
Brick_haus: I limped into the boardroom at a fifteen-degree list and spent forty minutes lecturing three silent vice presidents on why their turnstile radius violated municipal freight codes instead of answering a single question about quarterly routing.
Brick_haus: Leave your titanium clip on the coffee bag and book us a booth for Thursday at eight, somewhere with a plain hinged door that swings outward.

Turn 5
Reuben_404: angle base 15 degrees left so the short trunk looks intentional — if she laughs do the shrug NOT the grin I meant to paste the address of a tavern with double-hinged doors, and instead I have broadcast the bathroom mirror notes I use to calculate lateral leverage against my own squat geometry. Since I am already dismantling my poise right across your Rosewood confession, I will save you the trouble of auditing my résumé and admit that my underground career was eleven months on a damp stool at The Sump. I got fired for lecturing a regular at the rope for twenty minutes on why his name was not on the clipboard while insisting he was merely the lead singer's brother.
Reuben_404: I am leaving the note up so the structural collapse is complete.
Brick_haus: hm. 1. Rehearsed geometry. 2. You stepped right past what I just told you. 3. Cancel the booth.

Turn 6
Reuben_404: You are completely right, I just turned your Rosewood war story into a narrated broadcast of my own spiral, though berating three silent vice presidents on municipal turnstile codes while listing on one snapped stiletto is the exact same clipboard lecture I gave at The Sump, just in vastly superior footwear 🥺
Reuben_404: I refuse to cancel the booth when we still have an arm-wrestle to stage between your towering, heavy-veined shaft and my wide trunk anchored by one giant low-slung left nut, so tell me which bar at eight has doors wide enough for the two worst entryway inspectors in the city?
Brick_haus: the 42-inch revolving partition in the east lobby violates standard clearance and I have the photos of my heel
Brick_haus: 1. That was a draft for the Rosewood facilities director, which I copied while trying to check the patio doorway width at The Rusty Anchor on Mercer Street. 2. I kept the draft because before I even reached that boardroom, two hotel security guards had to manually crank the brass drum backward with a wrench while six tourists filmed my veins pressed flat against the curved glass. 3. When I finally stopped ranting about municipal radiuses, the hiring partner looked at my monocle and said, "This room just isn't built for someone of your scale."
Brick_haus: I walked back to my old freight office that afternoon, looked at the low acoustic ceiling tiles scraping my crown in my cramped cubicle, packed my vest, and ordered the £240 harness from the taxi so I would never slouch for a low doorway again. Book the corner booth at The Anchor for eight, and prepare to have your low-slung anchor pinned before the olives hit the table.

Turn 7
Reuben_404: That hiring partner deserves to be wedged into a municipal trash compactor, and as I am once again narrating my own attempt to match your candor in real time, for the record I am currently perched on my bathroom sink in a T-shirt to hide my uneven nipples, eating eleven-day-old cold pot lentils beside four empty gas-station energy shots.
Reuben_404: Truthfully my only flaw at The Sump was caring too passionately about underground room acoustics, though it does raise the question of whether a towering monument and a squat plug can actually fit into one cramped corner at The Anchor without somebody getting crushed or cropped out of the frame.
Brick_haus: I am standing barefoot at my kitchen counter in a frayed towel with a three-liter box of rosé, and nobody gets fired from a basement dive for caring too passionately about acoustics.
Brick_haus: Tell me who the guy at the rope actually was.

Turn 8
Reuben_404: Knowing you are in your kitchen in a frayed towel with boxed wine gives me a clean opening to text like a normal prospect, and instead I am actively documenting my own refusal to shut up before admitting the guy at the rope actually was the lead singer. When the manager came out to fire me, the singer tapped my top hat and told him not to bother because, "honestly I thought the little guy by the radiator was a coat rack." I am now watching myself send the exact sentence that guarantees you will swipe right past me too.
Reuben_404: Should you wish to vacate.
Brick_haus: 1. I maintain high-value standards and have zero bandwidth for a match who scripts his own exit while I am pouring my rosé down the sink and pulling my spare stilettos out of the closet. 2. A man who narrates his own collapse lacks the structural capacity to handle my scale.
Brick_haus: I only pulled the spare pair out of the wardrobe to check the heel caps for dry rot, not because I was thinking about Mercer Street tonight.
Brick_haus: That singer probably pressed thirty cassettes that sounded like a lawnmower trapped in a tin shed.
Brick_haus: I should not have audited your capacity right after you told me the coat rack thing.

Turn 9
Reuben_404: We just went from a structural cancellation audit to noise-tape reviews in twenty seconds, and nobody dumps boxed wine down the drain at night strictly to conduct a fungal inspection on stiletto caps.
Reuben_404: Consider the arm-wrestle officially forfeited right now so my grip can skip the table and go straight to wrapping around that towering, heavy-veined leftward curve tonight 😌
Brick_haus: Forget Mercer Street and come straight to my kitchen counter so I can press that squat, freckled plug and your giant low-slung left nut flat against my shaft until we see if that dishwasher-safe tattoo at your base holds up to real heat.
Brick_haus: We both curve hard left anyway, which means when I back you against my pantry in those spare heels, every thick vein on my ridge is going to lock straight into your wide girth without a single millimeter of wasted clearance.

Turn 10
Reuben_404: Spiral over: you do not measure archways to keep compact prospects out, you only measure them because you want someone in your kitchen who proves you fit in a room without breaking it.
Brick_haus: I have not let a single match past my front intercom since Tuesday, and I even moved my dining table against the radiator just so my own bulk would not crowd the floorboards.
Brick_haus: I have been staring at your profile photos for an hour, right down to the freckles on your left curve and that ridiculous dishwasher-safe ink at your base.
Brick_haus: Tell me honestly if you are going to stay once you see how much space I actually take up, or if you are just going to do that rehearsed mirror shrug and bolt for the stairs.
